Abstract ReMo, a training-free method, reduces inference input token cost for omni-modal large language models by compressing redundant visual tokens through cross-modal alignment and replacing object-level visual tokens with compact text descriptions, achieving comparable accuracy on audio-visual tasks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash The goal of this paper is to reduce the input token cost of Omni-modal large language models (Omni-LLMs) at inference time. Omni-LLMs reason jointly over audio, video and text, but the cost of the three streams is highly unbalanced: visual tokens account for the vast majority of the input, and are highly redundant. In this paper, we propose ReMo, a training-free framework that compresses visual tokens by redistributing their information across modalities: a visual token is kept only if its information appears nowhere else. ReMo achieves this in two ways: (i) it aligns audio and video in a shared space via closed-form calibration, and removes visual tokens already explained by the audio or by other visual tokens; and (ii) it replaces object-level visual tokens, identified by a lightweight object detector, with compact text proxies, short descriptions of each object and its location, conveying the same content in far fewer tokens. On Qwen2.5-Omni at two model scales, ReMo removes 54% of the input tokens with comparable accuracy. It performs comparably to the full-token model, reaching 101.2% and 101.4% of its average accuracy over five audio-visual benchmarks.

Abstract  Reinforcement learning from terminal answer rewards enables search-augmented language models to interleave reasoning and retrieval without step-level annotations, yet outcome supervision assigns identical credit to every search in a trajectory regardless of whether it retrieved useful information. This limitation is especially severe when all sampled rollouts for a question fail, collapsing group-centered outcome advantages to zero even when trajectories gather markedly different evidence. We present Evidence-based Credit Assignment (ECA), which augments terminal outcome rewards with a training-only auxiliary signal measuring how retrieved documents improve the likelihood of a reference answer. Rather than scoring the full dialogue prefix, ECA queries a frozen snapshot of the current policy under an evidence-only prompt containing solely the question and the deduplicated documents observed so far, isolating the utility of retrieved passages from surface variations in generated thoughts or search queries. Within this framework, we compare two return formulations, ECA-Step (which accumulates step-clipped potential increments) and ECA-Horizon (which preserves the telescoping horizon gain in potential), paired with cross-fold group normalization, novelty gating, and search-local token assignment. Across seven Search QA benchmarks, ECA-Horizon improves strict macro exact match over an outcome-only GRPO baseline by 1.03 and 4.80 percentage points on Qwen2.5-3B- and 7B-Instruct, respectively, outperforming both RLOO and GiGPO. Zero-shot evaluation on MultiHop-RAG further demonstrates strong transfer to multi-hop inference queries.

Проблема в том, что обычный outcome-based GRPO присваивает одинаковую награду всем поисковым запросам траектории, и при провале всех rollout'ов advantage обнуляется. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Reinforcement learning from terminal answer rewards enables search-augmented language models to interleave reasoning and retrieval without step-level annotations, yet outcome supervision assigns identical credit to every search in a trajectory regardless of whether it retrieved useful information. This limitation is especially severe when all sampled rollouts for a question fail, collapsing group-centered outcome advantages to zero even when trajectories gather markedly different evidence. We present Evidence-based Credit Assignment (ECA), which augments terminal outcome rewards with a training-only auxiliary signal measuring how retrieved documents improve the likelihood of a reference answer. Rather than scoring the full dialogue prefix, ECA queries a frozen snapshot of the current policy under an evidence-only prompt containing solely the question and the deduplicated documents observed so far, isolating the utility of retrieved passages from surface variations in generated thoughts or search queries. Within this framework, we compare two return formulations, ECA-Step (which accumulates step-clipped potential increments) and ECA-Horizon (which preserves the telescoping horizon gain in potential), paired with cross-fold group normalization, novelty gating, and search-local token assignment. Across seven Search QA benchmarks, ECA-Horizon improves strict macro exact match over an outcome-only GRPO baseline by 1.03 and 4.80 percentage points on Qwen2.5-3B- and 7B-Instruct, respectively, outperforming both RLOO and GiGPO. Zero-shot evaluation on MultiHop-RAG further demonstrates strong transfer to multi-hop inference queries."

Abstract  Visible artifacts are an unreliable cue for detecting generated videos, because newer generators learn to remove them. We instead detect a trace left by the generation pipeline itself. Most video generators decode latents with a 3D causal VAE that groups consecutive frames in time, so the fidelity of generated frames rises and falls periodically with their position in a group. We call this periodic trace VAE Rhythm. Real videos are captured frame by frame and do not have it. To reveal it without training, we propose a phase scan. The scan reconstructs a clip with an off-the-shelf VAE from successive start offsets and measures the periodicity of the reconstruction error across these offsets. We find VAE Rhythm in most of the 39 variants of 31 generators we test, including the closed-source Sora, Kling 3.0 and Seedance 2.0. We use the rhythm as a zero-shot score, as a plug-in for existing detectors and as a data engine that pairs real videos with their VAE reconstructions to train our detector CaDet. On DF26, the training-free rhythm score outperforms all 15 retrained baselines, and as a plug-in it improves all 19 tested detectors by 6.7% to 23.7% AUROC. CaDet achieves the best AUROC on AIGVDBench, DF26 and three 2026 closed-source models, and its variant trained without any generated video still exceeds 90% on AIGVDBench.

Большинство генераторов декодируют латенты через 3D causal VAE, который группирует соседние кадры, поэтому качество кадров periodically меняется в зависимости от позиции в группе; это явление авторы называют VAE Rhythm. [19.11.2026 11:10] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Abstract  Thinking with images empowers vision-language models (VLMs) to use an external Zoom-in tool for fine-grained visual search. Recent works employ supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL) with tool-specific designs to enable such capabilities. However, achieving logical reasoning alongside accurate and efficient tool use remains a significant challenge. To address this, we shift the focus away from increasing RL complexity and instead identify SFT data quality as a key bottleneck. We then propose LogT (short for Logically Think with Images), a fully automated SFT data engine that explicitly enforces logic-chain-guided reasoning. LogT generates an SFT dataset featuring challenging, visually grounded questions and structured tool-use traces with human-like logic. We validate our approach by fine-tuning two base models, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and InternVL3.5-8B, on the dataset and evaluating them across seven benchmark test sets. We show that LogT-SFT based on Qwen2.5-VL-7B outperforms the state-of-the-art trained with both SFT and RL, despite using 3.1times less data and >120times fewer GPU hours. Furthermore, applying a simple RL recipe without tool-specific design on LogT-SFT yields a model that surpasses prior methods with complex RL setups by a substantial 9.59-point margin. In addition to strong performance gains, our model exhibits higher tool-use accuracy and efficiency, as well as more structured and logically consistent reasoning traces, directly validating the effectiveness of our approach.
[19.11.2026 11:10] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен LogT — полностью автоматизированный движок генерации данных для SFT, который учит vision-language модели (VLM) рассуждать с изображениями и использовать внешний инструмент приближения (Zoom-in). Авторы показали, что ключевым узким местом является качество SFT-данных, а не сложность RL, и создали датасет с визуально обоснованными вопросами и логическими цепочками использования инструмента. Модели Qwen2.5-VL-7B и InternVL3.5-8B, дообученные на этих данных, превосходят state-of-the-art методы, обученные с SFT и RL, при использовании в 3.1 раза меньше данных и более чем в 120 раз меньше GPU-часов. Простой RL без специального дизайна под инструмент поверх LogT-SFT даёт прирост в 9.59 пункта, при этом модель показывает более точное и эффективное использование инструмента и логически согласованные цепочки рассуждений.", "emoji": "🔍", "title": "Качественные данные важнее сложного RL: логика и зум для визуального поиска"}

To address this, we shift the focus away from increasing RL complexity and instead identify SFT data quality as a key bottleneck. We then propose LogT (short for Logically Think with Images), a fully automated SFT data engine that explicitly enforces logic-chain-guided reasoning. LogT generates an SFT dataset featuring challenging, visually grounded questions and structured tool-use traces with human-like logic. We validate our approach by fine-tuning two base models, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and InternVL3.5-8B, on the dataset and evaluating them across seven benchmark test sets. We show that LogT-SFT based on Qwen2.5-VL-7B outperforms the state-of-the-art trained with both SFT and RL, despite using 3.1times less data and >120times fewer GPU hours. Furthermore, applying a simple RL recipe without tool-specific design on LogT-SFT yields a model that surpasses prior methods with complex RL setups by a substantial 9.59-point margin. In addition to strong performance gains, our model exhibits higher tool-use accuracy and efficiency, as well as more structured and logically consistent reasoning traces, directly validating the effectiveness of our approach."

Abstract A multi-view debiasing method aggregates within-class partitions across diverse representation spaces to reduce reliance on spurious correlations and improve worst-group accuracy without bias supervision.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Deep models often rely on spurious correlations that fail on underrepresented groups. Existing methods either correct human-specified bias attributes or infer unknown biases from the learned representation space. However, annotated attributes may differ from the shortcuts actually used by a model, while a single representation provides incomplete and potentially noisy evidence. Our key insight is that a bias obscured in one feature space may be clearer in another. In this work, we propose CHORD (Cross-view Harmonic Outlier Reversal for Debiasing), a multi-view framework that allows samples to "sing in chorus" across diverse representation spaces to achieve robust cross-view consensus. CHORD aggregates aggregates within-class partitions in each view, which is a proxy for specifying candidate biases, to debias the target representation and classifier without bias supervision. Experiments on seven benchmarks across vision, language, speech, and medical imaging show consistent improvements in worst-group accuracy. Code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/davelee-uestc/chord_debiasing_public .

Вместо использования размеченных атрибутов смещения метод анализирует несколько пространств признаков (multi-view) и выделяет внутриклассовые разбиения, которые служат кандидатами на скрытые bias. Затем эти разбиения агрегируются, чтобы скорректировать целевое представление и классификатор без надзора за смещениями. Abstract AnisoFlow learns an anisotropic, learnable prediction target for flow-based generative models, improving FID and image quality without altering the architecture or sampler.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Flow-based generative models are trained on a hand-chosen prediction target, such as the velocity, the noise, or the clean data. This choice strongly affects training and sample quality. We introduce Anisotropic Flow Models (AnisoFlow), which replaces a fixed prediction target with a fully learnable one. AnisoFlow learns to scale the noise component of the target anisotropically, assigning a separate nonnegative weight to each direction in a jointly learned basis. A closed-form map recovers the velocity from the network's prediction, leaving the architecture, flow-matching loss, and sampler unchanged. Since recovery is exact, training optimizes the target purely to make it easier for the network to predict. We theoretically analyze the resulting prediction error and predict that the preferred noise weight in each direction scales near-linearly with data variance, in agreement with the weights learned empirically in pixel space. AnisoFlow improves FID from 1.90 to 1.55 over the JiT baseline on ImageNet 256times256, by learning only the prediction target, without representation alignment or architectural changes. In pixel-space text-to-image generation, AnisoFlow improves image quality and, through better-conditioned velocity recovery, largely removes patch-seam artifacts under reduced-precision inference.
[19.11.2026 11:10] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает AnisoFlow — метод для flow-based генеративных моделей, в котором фиксированная цель предсказания заменяется полностью обучаемой. Модель учит анизотропные весовые коэффициенты для компоненты шума в обученном базисе, а замкнутая формула восстанавливает velocity из предсказания сети, поэтому архитектура, loss flow-matching и sampler не меняются. Теоретически показано, что оптимальный вес шума по каждому направлению почти линейно зависит от дисперсии данных, что подтверждается экспериментально в пиксельном пространстве. На ImageNet 256×256 метод улучшает FID с 1.90 до 1.55 относительно базовой модели JiT, а в text-to-image генерации уменьшает артефакты на стыках патчей при инференсе в пониженной точности.", "emoji": "🎯", "title": "Учим модель, что именно предсказывать, — и генерация становится лучше"}

Abstract BiCAT shares a motion representation across arms and conditions each arm's tokens on its partner via symmetric cross-arm attention and a joint decoder, enabling parallel arm token prediction and improving bimanual manipulation success.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Autoregressive vision-language-action (VLA) models predict discrete action tokens with the next-token objective of their vision-language backbone, so the action tokenizer defines the interface between the policy and the robot. In bimanual manipulation, the two arms share a repertoire of movements yet coordinate through complementary roles, and an action representation should capture both. However, most existing tokenizers encode both arms as a single concatenated action vector, in which motion patterns common to the two arms are not shared and cross-arm dependencies remain entangled within each token. The policy must therefore learn both aspects implicitly. We propose Bimanual Coordination Action Tokenization (BiCAT), which shares one motion representation across arms while conditioning each arm's tokens on its partner through symmetric cross-arm attention and a joint decoder. The policy then predicts all tokens of each arm in parallel, generating the left arm first and the right arm conditioned on it. Across ten RoboTwin 2.0 tasks and six real-world tasks on three embodiments, BiCAT achieves the highest success among the compared tokenizers. In simulation, it improves mean success by 17.6 percentage points over the strongest of eight baselines, with the largest gains on tasks that require close coordination between the arms, while requiring only 40.5 ms per action chunk. These results suggest that the action tokenizer is a natural place to model bimanual coordination.
[19.11.2026 11:11] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается BiCAT — метод токенизации действий для двуручной манипуляции в autoregressive VLA моделях. Он использует общее представление движений для обеих рук и симметричное cross-arm attention, чтобы токены каждой руки учитывали партнёрскую. Модель предсказывает токены обеих рук параллельно, сначала левой, затем правой с учётом левой. На задачах RoboTwin 2.0 и реальных роботах BiCAT превосходит существующие токенизаторы, особенно там, где нужна тесная координация рук.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Координация рук начинается с токенизации действий"}

"Abstract BiCAT shares a motion representation across arms and conditions each arm's tokens on its partner via symmetric cross-arm attention and a joint decoder, enabling parallel arm token prediction and improving bimanual manipulation success.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Autoregressive vision-language-action (VLA) models predict discrete action tokens with the next-token objective of their vision-language backbone, so the action tokenizer defines the interface between the policy and the robot. In bimanual manipulation, the two arms share a repertoire of movements yet coordinate through complementary roles, and an action representation should capture both. However, most existing tokenizers encode both arms as a single concatenated action vector, in which motion patterns common to the two arms are not shared and cross-arm dependencies remain entangled within each token. The policy must therefore learn both aspects implicitly. We propose Bimanual Coordination Action Tokenization (BiCAT), which shares one motion representation across arms while conditioning each arm's tokens on its partner through symmetric cross-arm attention and a joint decoder. Abstract A prototype-guided progressive obfuscation method preserves relative item relations while perturbing high-influence semantic embeddings to defend against inversion attacks in LLM-enhanced recommender systems.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash LLM-enhanced recommender systems encode item side information into semantic embeddings, but these embeddings may leak sensitive textual attributes through inversion attacks. Existing input-side defenses rely on trusted collection or local token perturbation, often disrupting the inter-item semantic topology needed for recommendation. To address it, we introduce relational fidelity as the key objective, requiring semantic shift for privacy while preserving relative item relations for utility. Based on this principle, we propose prototype-guided progressive obfuscation (PGPO), which learns topology-aware obfuscation anchors for high-influence prototype words and propagates them through a static semantic graph. PGPO is optimized with GRPO using individual dissimilarity, structural consistency, and semantic constraint rewards. Experiments on two benchmarks show strong inversion defense with near-upper-bound recommendation performance. The code is available at https://github.com/Shengxiang-Lin/PGPO.

Авторы вводят принцип relational fidelity: эмбеддинги должны смещаться достаточно для приватности, но сохранять относительные отношения между товарами для качества рекомендаций. Abstract AnyS1 converts procedural skills into shared decision points, trains a small calibrated pointer model to resolve routine choices, and falls back to a large model when uncertain, reducing cost and latency while preserving reliability.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Autonomous LLM agents incur substantial cost and latency by invoking a large model (System-2) at every step, even when many intermediate actions choose among a small set of known candidates. Existing dual-system agents offload such decisions to a lightweight System-1 model, but rely on workflow-specific hooks authored per environment. We present AnyS1, a framework that turns human-authored procedural skills, whose instructions naturally specify where choices arise and which options apply, into a shared, domain-general source of decision points. Offline, AnyS1 mines grounded enumerable decisions across over 1,000 public skills, gathers execution-grounded trajectory states inside isolated containers to construct Any-DP, and trains a single calibrated 4B pointer System-1 with label smoothing. At run time, compiled workflows resolve routine decisions in one forward pass and fall back to System-2 when confidence falls below a calibrated threshold. Across three unseen benchmarks and distinct System-2 model families, AnyS1 preserves task reliability while cutting episode cost by 75-91% and model-call time by 63-92% relative to an uncompiled System-2 agent, and maintains performance under zero-shot threshold transfer.

Небольшая калиброванная модель-указатель на 4B параметров, обученная с label smoothing, решает рутинные выборы за один прямой проход, а при низкой уверенности передаёт задачу большой LLM (System-2). Такой подход снижает стоимость и задержку работы автономных LLM-агентов на 75–91% и 63–92% соответственно, сохраняя надёжность выполнения задач. Abstract Chorus shares one set of n-gram embedding tables across all layers, letting each layer project and rescale the embedding using its hidden state, achieving better quality with much smaller tables and lower optimizer memory.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Language models can gain capacity at little computational cost from n-gram embedding tables: large tables of learned vectors for short sequences of consecutive tokens, which are looked up and added to the model's input or to its intermediate layers. Recent designs commonly use a separate n-gram embedding table for each layer, which makes the tables costly in memory and prevents layers from sharing what they learn. Sharing a single table, however, gives every layer the same embedding and leaves no room for layer-wise preferences, and it performs poorly in practice. We propose Chorus, in which one set of n-gram embedding tables serves the whole network and every layer reads it in its own way. Each layer maps the refined embedding through its own projection layer, after rescaling its components by factors computed from its current hidden state. Chorus matches the quality of existing n-gram methods with 87% smaller tables: with 1.2B table parameters it predicts text better than FactorEngram and DeepSeek's Engram do with 9.6B. It outperforms Engram, TN-gram and FactorEngram in every setting where we compared them, across table sizes from 20M to 9.6B parameters. We also show that the recently proposed memory-efficient optimizer Ember works well with Chorus, saving 99.2% of table-optimizer memory compared with AdamW without degrading training performance. These results enable further scaling of n-gram embedding tables along the table-size axis by reducing memory use and allowing shared knowledge to be reused across layers.
[19.11.2026 11:11] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается метод Chorus, в котором одна общая таблица n-gram эмбеддингов используется всеми слоями нейросети вместо отдельной таблицы для каждого слоя. Каждый слой проецирует уточнённый эмбеддинг через собственный projection layer, предварительно масштабируя его компоненты с помощью коэффициентов, вычисляемых из текущего hidden state. Метод достигает качества существующих n-gram подходов при таблицах, меньших на 87%, и превосходит Engram, TN-gram и FactorEngram при размерах от 20M до 9.6B параметров. Также показано, что оптимизатор Ember с Chorus экономит 99.2% памяти оптимизатора для таблиц по сравнению с AdamW без ухудшения обучения.", "emoji": "🎶", "title": "Один общий словарь n-gram эмбеддингов для всех слоёв: меньше памяти, лучше качество"}

Abstract UltraTex employs a cascaded multi-view framework that integrates physical knowledge and vision-language model features to accurately generate physically plausible PBR texture maps.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Generating physically based rendering (PBR) texture maps for a 3D mesh from a single reference image is highly ambiguous, as the observed appearance entangles material identity, surface reflectance, and illumination. While current generative methods produce realistic albedo maps, they still struggle to infer accurate metallic and roughness attributes for relighting. We present UltraTex, a cascaded multi-view framework that moves beyond appearance matching by integrating physical knowledge into multi-view diffusion-based PBR generation. UltraTex first jointly predicts albedo and world-space shading normals to generate more detailed albedo maps, and then predicts metallic-roughness (MR) maps conditioned on the generated albedo, reference image, and material semantic priors. To inject material semantics, a lightweight adapter transforms hidden-layer features of a vision-language model (VLM) into intrinsic material representation tokens, providing part-level knowledge anchors for MR estimation. To model material-light interaction, photometric representation alignment guides intermediate diffusion features toward ground-truth material renderings under controlled lighting. This makes metallic and roughness variations observable through highlight intensity, specular sharpness, and local reflectance contrast, providing stronger guidance for fine-grained material prediction without explicit lighting supervision. Extensive experiments show that UltraTex generates high-quality textures and outperforms existing open-source and commercial methods in visual fidelity, material accuracy, and physical plausibility.
[19.11.2026 11:11] Response: {"desc": "UltraTex — это каскадный мультиракурсный фреймворк, который генерирует PBR-текстуры (albedo, metallic и roughness) для 3D-меша по одному референсному изображению. Сначала модель совместно предсказывает albedo и нормали в мировых координатах, а затем на их основе оценивает карты metallic-roughness. Для учёта материалов используется lightweight adapter, который переводит скрытые признаки VLM в токены материальных представлений, а photometric representation alignment направляет промежуточные признаки диффузионной модели к реалистичному рендерингу без явного обучения на освещении. Эксперименты показывают, что по качеству, точности материалов и физической правдоподобности UltraTex превосходит как открытые, так и коммерческие аналоги.", "emoji": "🧱", "title": "Физически правдоподобные текстуры для 3D по одному фото"}

"Abstract UltraTex employs a cascaded multi-view framework that integrates physical knowledge and vision-language model features to accurately generate physically plausible PBR texture maps.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Generating physically based rendering (PBR) texture maps for a 3D mesh from a single reference image is highly ambiguous, as the observed appearance entangles material identity, surface reflectance, and illumination. While current generative methods produce realistic albedo maps, they still struggle to infer accurate metallic and roughness attributes for relighting. We present UltraTex, a cascaded multi-view framework that moves beyond appearance matching by integrating physical knowledge into multi-view diffusion-based PBR generation. UltraTex first jointly predicts albedo and world-space shading normals to generate more detailed albedo maps, and then predicts metallic-roughness (MR) maps conditioned on the generated albedo, reference image, and material semantic priors. To inject material semantics, a lightweight adapter transforms hidden-layer features of a vision-language model (VLM) into intrinsic material representation tokens, providing part-level knowledge anchors for MR estimation. To model material-light interaction, photometric representation alignment guides intermediate diffusion features toward ground-truth material renderings under controlled lighting. This makes metallic and roughness variations observable through highlight intensity, specular sharpness, and local reflectance contrast, providing stronger guidance for fine-grained material prediction without explicit lighting supervision. Extensive experiments show that UltraTex generates high-quality textures and outperforms existing open-source and commercial methods in visual fidelity, material accuracy, and physical plausibility."

Abstract A novel framework addresses the limitations of current reinforcement learning-based red-teaming methods by employing dictionary learning and on-policy adversarial training to improve the robustness of LLM agents against indirect prompt injection attacks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Large language model (LLM) agents routinely interact with untrusted external data, exposing them to indirect prompt injection (IPI) attacks that hijack agents into executing attacker-chosen tasks. To uncover these vulnerabilities at scale, automated red-teaming methods often leverage reinforcement learning (RL) to generate attacks that achieve high attack success rates (ASR). Model developers use the attacks discovered during internal red-teaming to harden agents through adversarial training before deployment, a collaborative offensive-defensive process known in cybersecurity as purple-teaming. However, we show that achieving high ASR during red-teaming does not guarantee robustness gains from subsequent adversarial training. Specifically, we find that two state-of-the-art RL-based red-teaming methods, RL-Hammer and PISmith, suffer from mode collapse, repeatedly discovering similar attack strategies whose additional successes add no additional robustness improvements to adversarial training. To address this gap, we introduce PIPETTE, a unified prompt injection purple-teaming framework that effectively converts red-teaming discoveries into defender robustness improvements. PIPETTE employs a dictionary learning-based reward that encourages broader coverage of attack strategies during red-teaming. It also integrates an on-policy adversarial training pipeline tailored to IPI defense, enabling agents to resist attacks while completing legitimate user tasks. Extensive evaluations across agentic benchmarks (AgentDojo and ASB) demonstrate that PIPETTE produces defenders that achieve near-zero attack success rates on static attacks and can withstand strong adaptive attacks, while maintaining benign task utility close to that of the undefended baseline.
[19.11.2026 11:11] Response: {"desc": "Статья показывает, что методы автоматического red-teaming на основе reinforcement learning, такие как RL-Hammer и PISmith, страдают от mode collapse: они находят похожие атаки, и обучение на них не повышает устойчивость агентов. Авторы предлагают PIPETTE — фреймворк для purple-teaming, где reward на основе dictionary learning поощряет разнообразие стратегий атак. Также используется on-policy adversarial training, который учит LLM-агентов защищаться от indirect prompt injection и выполнять легитимные задачи. Эксперименты на AgentDojo и ASB показывают почти нулевой ASR на статических атаках и устойчивость к адаптивным атакам при сохранении полезности агента.", "emoji": "🛡️", "title": "Разнообразие атак — ключ к надёжной защите LLM-агентов"}

Abstract  Fine-grained questions about long videos ask how many times an action occurs, in what order events unfold, or exactly when a state changes. Answering them can require finding brief moments scattered across hours of footage. Agentic models that decide when and where to look promise to find these moments, yet this ability is hard both to measure and to learn. Sparse supporting evidence may establish that an action occurred without establishing its total count; verifying timeline-wide claims requires checking for missed occurrences and double counting. Answer-only supervision, in turn, does not explicitly supervise the search for evidence. We present NexSight, which builds verifiable tasks to both evaluate and train agentic long-video understanding. Its agentic pipeline, SightForge, accepts a task only if a verifier blind to the draft answer can reconstruct it from evidence densely reacquired from the raw video and counterexample checks fail to refute it. This yields SightLine, 310 evidence-traceable tasks from 108 long videos, evaluated through SightLoop, a shared multiscale harness supporting temporal exploration, local inspection, evidence integration, and revisiting while recording returned observations. On disjoint videos, solver trajectories on verified tasks become step-level supervision for observation decisions. The strongest model reaches only 54.52% on SightLine, yet 79.35% when given the annotated evidence. With under 6K examples, Sight-35B nearly doubles its base model's interactive accuracy (21.29% to 42.26%), outperforms the 397B model of its family, begins using explicit spatial inspection after post-training, which its base model never uses, and gains over 8 points on two external long-video benchmarks.
[19.11.2026 11:11] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет NexSight — фреймворк для создания проверяемых задач на понимание длинных видео, где агентная LLM-модель должна находить разрозненные короткие фрагменты для ответа на вопросы о количестве, порядке и моментах изменения событий. Конвейер SightForge отбирает задачи только тогда, когда независимый верификатор, не видящий черновой ответ, может восстановить их из плотно повторно извлечённых кадров, а контрпримеры не опровергают ответ. Получившийся бенчмарк SightLine (310 задач на 108 видео) оценивается через SightLoop, а траектории решений используются как пошаговый supervision для обучения решений о наблюдении. Модель Sight-35B, дообученная менее чем на 6 тысячах примеров, почти удваивает точность базовой модели и превосходит более крупную модель своего семейства, сохраняя улучшения на внешних бенчмарках.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Учим агентную модель искать нужные кадры в длинном видео, а не просто отвечать на вопрос"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Fine-grained questions about long videos ask how many times an action occurs, in what order events unfold, or exactly when a state changes. Answering them can require finding brief moments scattered across hours of footage. Agentic models that decide when and where to look promise to find these moments, yet this ability is hard both to measure and to learn. Sparse supporting evidence may establish that an action occurred without establishing its total count; verifying timeline-wide claims requires checking for missed occurrences and double counting. Answer-only supervision, in turn, does not explicitly supervise the search for evidence. We present NexSight, which builds verifiable tasks to both evaluate and train agentic long-video understanding. Its agentic pipeline, SightForge, accepts a task only if a verifier blind to the draft answer can reconstruct it from evidence densely reacquired from the raw video and counterexample checks fail to refute it. This yields SightLine, 310 evidence-traceable tasks from 108 long videos, evaluated through SightLoop, a shared multiscale harness supporting temporal exploration, local inspection, evidence integration, and revisiting while recording returned observations. On disjoint videos, solver trajectories on verified tasks become step-level supervision for observation decisions. The strongest model reaches only 54.52% on SightLine, yet 79.35% when given the annotated evidence. With under 6K examples, Sight-35B nearly doubles its base model's interactive accuracy (21.29% to 42.26%), outperforms the 397B model of its family, begins using explicit spatial inspection after post-training, which its base model never uses, and gains over 8 points on two external long-video benchmarks."

Abstract A new metric for automatic CAD generation evaluates models through engineering-drawing projections with a saturating penalty along curves, exposing small local errors that volume- or surface-based metrics miss and enabling use as a reinforcement learning reward or test-time verifier.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Automatic CAD generation is commonly evaluated with volumetric IoU, Chamfer Distance, and surface F-score, which barely penalize local errors that occupy only a small fraction of a part's volume or surface area, such as a misplaced hole or a slot on the wrong face, even though such errors are critical to manufacturing. We formalize these metrics in a unified integral form and show that the weight of a local error decays as the (k+q)-th power of its size, where k is the dimension of the integration domain and q is determined by the penalty function. Guided by this analysis, we propose DrawScore, which projects both models into three orthographic views with visible and hidden lines, as in engineering drawings, and applies a saturating penalty along the projected curves, so that the weight of a local error decays only linearly. Controlled experiments confirm the predicted decay orders, and on outputs of existing CAD generation agents, DrawScore exposes errors in outputs that IoU rates as nearly perfect. DrawScore further improves generation as a reinforcement learning reward and, for CAD generation from engineering drawings, serves as a test-time verifier without 3D ground truth or an additional reward model. Our code is available at https://github.com/UESTC-Simon/DrawScore.
[19.11.2026 11:12] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает новую метрику DrawScore для оценки автоматической генерации CAD-моделей. Модели проецируются в три ортогональных вида с видимыми и скрытыми линиями, как на инженерных чертежах, и штраф применяется вдоль проекций. Авторы показывают, что привычные метрики IoU, Chamfer Distance и F-score почти не замечают малые локальные ошибки, а DrawScore их выявляет. Abstract A foundation model fine-tuned from a large language model measures and predicts human behaviour and aligns simulated population responses with human statistics, improving accuracy over frontier LLMs.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to infer psychological attributes, predict individual behaviour, and simulate human populations. However, despite generating plausible responses, they often misrepresent individuals and distort population statistics. Here we introduce Chiron, a foundation model to measure and predict human behaviour. We jointly fine-tune an LLM to represent psychological constructs, predict individual behaviour, and align population response distributions with human statistics. The training and evaluation data span 36 behavioural domains and 78 social science experiments, comprising 288,307 person records, 1,411,750 atomic behavioural decisions, and 99,953 psychometric assessments over 109 psychological constructs. In our evaluations, Chiron's psychological measurements agree more closely with human self-reports and exhibit fewer biases that undermine psychometric validity. It predicts individual behaviour more accurately than frontier LLMs (up to 28.4% relative improvement). In simulations of social science experiments, it more accurately captures the direction and magnitude of human experimental effects (82.2--94.3% lower effect-size estimation error). Together, these results establish Chiron as a unified computational instrument for measuring and predicting human behaviour.

Модель совместно обучается представлять психологические конструкты, предсказывать индивидуальное поведение и выравнивать распределения ответов симулированной популяции с человеческой статистикой. Обучение и оценка проведены на 36 поведенческих доменах, 78 социальных экспериментах и более чем 288 тысячах записей людей. Abstract Across three shopping benchmarks, increasing large language model shopping agents' thinking effort did not statistically improve product-choice quality but significantly raised reported confidence, and most models were poorly calibrated with correlated errors.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed as autonomous shopping agents that process multimodal product listings (consisting of visual and text data) and recommend purchases on behalf of consumers. Naturally, one might assume that models with more thinking power would make higher-quality purchasing decisions. To test this belief, we ask two questions about such agents: does asking a model to think harder make it choose better products, and does the confidence it reports tell us the likelihood it may be wrong? We benchmark ten frontier models on three product-choice benchmarks built from WebShop, Amazon's SQID and Wayfair's WANDS platforms to see if LLMs with more thinking power actually make better-quality product purchase decisions. Across all three benchmarks, we found that increasing the thinking effort does not increase decision quality in a statistically meaningful way. The same change in thinking effort raises stated confidence at a statistically significant level, so extra thinking buys confidence rather than correctness. We also measure the calibration of these models (how well their reported confidence percentage matches their true correctness percentage) in these tasks and show that most models are not well-calibrated at every effort level. We also find that errors are strongly shared: when one model errs in a task, several others tend to err on that same task. Furthermore, on tasks where several models all err, a large number of them pick the same wrong product.
[19.11.2026 11:12] Response: {"desc": "Авторы исследуют, улучшает ли увеличение «глубины размышлений» качество выбора товаров у LLM-агентов для покупок, которые обрабатывают мультимодальные карточки товаров. Тестировались десять передовых моделей на трёх бенчмарках, построенных на основе WebShop, SQID от Amazon и WANDS от Wayfair. Результаты показали, что рост thinking effort статистически значимо не повышает качество решений, но заметно увеличивает заявленную уверенность модели. Кроме того, большинство моделей плохо откалиброваны, а их ошибки сильно коррелированы: часто они ошибаются на одних и тех же задачах и выбирают один и тот же неверный товар.", "emoji": "🛒", "title": "Больше размышлений — больше уверенности, но не больше правоты"}

Abstract Adaptive Visual-Textual Reasoning combines generative visual reflection with textual reasoning and learns to route between them per input, improving multimodal task accuracy while reducing inference latency.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Vision-Language Models increasingly rely on textual reasoning to solve advanced multimodal tasks. Despite its effectiveness, this paradigm faces two fundamental limitations. First, relying on textual reasoning alone can limit perceptual ability in vision-centric questions, where accurate answers depend on interpreting task-relevant visual evidence. Second, applying textual reasoning uniformly across inputs overlooks question-dependent variation in both reasoning modality and depth, often producing verbose and unnecessary traces when little logical inference is required. To this end, we propose Adaptive Visual-Textual Reasoning (AVTR), which introduces visual imagination as a complementary reasoning modality to textual thinking, enabling the model to revisit its visual observations and distill the salient evidence through generative visual reflection. Furthermore, we formulate reasoning-modality selection as a learned routing problem, allowing a single policy to adaptively harness the complementary strengths of visual reflection and textual reasoning in response to the perceptual and inferential demands of the input. Across 16 benchmarks, AVTR improves the average score by 4.5 points over Qwen3.5 baseline, while reducing inference latency by 33\%. It also scales effectively into a smaller size backbone, outperforming the evaluated 2B-scale baselines.
[19.11.2026 11:12] Response: {"desc": "Авторы предлагают Adaptive Visual-Textual Reasoning (AVTR) — подход для Vision-Language Models, который дополняет текстовые рассуждения визуальным воображением. Модель может возвращаться к визуальным наблюдениям и выделять ключевые признаки через generative visual reflection. Выбор модальности рассуждений формулируется как задача learned routing, где единая policy адаптивно решает, использовать ли визуальную рефлексию или текстовый reasoning.
